General formalism, classification, and demystification of the current warp-drive spacetimes
Hamed Barzegar, Thomas Buchert, Quentin Vigneron

Abstract
We critically examine proposals for the so-called warp-drive spacetimes and classify these models based on their various restrictions within the framework of General Relativity. We then provide a summary of general formalism for each class, and in the process, we highlight some misconceptions, misunderstandings, and errors in the literature that have been used to support claims about the physicality and feasibility of these models. On the way, we prove several new no-go theorems. Our analysis shows that when the principles of General Relativity are applied correctly, most claims regarding physical warp drives must be reassessed, and it becomes highly challenging to justify or support the viability of such models, not merely due to the violation of energy conditions.



I
Introduction

The human drive to explore and travel faster meets a fundamental barrier in Special Relativity (SR): the
light-cone barrier
. However, this has not stopped physicists to explore possible workarounds for fast and eventually faster-than-light (FTL) travels. This goes back to efforts within SR, started, to our knowledge, first by
McMillan 1957
,
1
1

1

McMillan was an American physicist who shared the 1951 Nobel Prize in Chemistry with Glenn Seaborg for the discovery of the neptunium, whereas his nephew, John Clauser, was awarded the 2022 Nobel Prize in Physics, jointly with Alain Aspect and Anton Zeilinger.

using the relativistic effects of the so-called
hyperbolic motion
resulted from a uniform acceleration. In his example,
McMillan 1957
shows that a uniformly accelerated observer (spaceship) can travel “1.2 billion light-years in only 21 years” as measured by an observer on, say, Earth (see also ruminations by
Mumford 2021
).
This, later, motivated
Rindler 1960
to generalize the hyperbolic motion to the curved spacetime, after whom the
Rindler coordinates
are coined. However, the apparent superluminal travel is a consequence of time dilation of the uniformly accelerated observers, and not the breaking of the light-cone barrier, and both conclude that such travels are impossible because insurmountable energy costs and cosmological horizon render the prospect of such travel “slight” and “cosmologically quite insignificant”
Rindler 1960
;
McMillan 1957
.

The idea of FTL travel entered General Relativity (GR) through the concept of
wormholes
, based on the works by Flamm
Flamm 1916
;
*2015_Flamm_Republication
, who first described a “tunnel” geometry joining two asymptotically flat surfaces of spacetime, and
Einstein and Rosen 1935
, who proposed a “bridge” as a model for elementary particles. This early foundation led to the work of
Wheeler 1955
, who explored multiply-connected spacetimes through hypothesized
geons
, and the paper by
Misner and Wheeler 1957
that first coined the term “wormhole” to describe mass and electric charge as properties of empty curved space (geometry).
These conceptual developments eventually led to the first specific models for traversability in 1973, such as Homer Ellis’s “drainhole”
Ellis 1973
and Bronnikov’s tunnel-like solutions
Bronnikov 1973
. A modern “renaissance” in the field was triggered by
Morris and Thorne 1988
, who defined the formal requirements for traversable wormholes to be used in interstellar travel, specifically identifying the need for exotic matter that violates the null energy condition (NEC) to hold the throat open (see
Visser 1996
;
Lobo 2017
;
Lobo 2008
for a more detailed account of the historical development).

Following the proposal of wormholes, the next step in conception of FTL travels in GR was the invention of the
warp drive
in 1994 by
Alcubierre 1994
,
2
2

2

Alcubierre never pursued his own idea further (except from the review work
Alcubierre and Lobo 2017
), which may indicate that he was in agreement with the philosophical perspective outlined in
Section

V
, a view we also share.
which allows for superluminal motion, supposedly, by contracting spacetime in front of a bubble and expanding it behind (see
Section

III.8
below).
A motivation for Alcubierre’s idea can be drawn from cosmology, specifically the inflationary phase of the early universe that causally connects two observers who should not have been in causal contact in a standard Big-Bang scenario without inflation
Alcubierre 1994
;
Barzegar and Buchert 2025
.
The main characteristic idea of Alcubierre is that, in contrast to the cosmological case, only a localized part of the universe would causing such effect.
Because this original model required physically impossible amounts of exotic matter,
Broeck 1999
proposed a modification that significantly reduced the (supposed) necessary negative energy density (see
Section

III.5.1
below).
Natário 2002
advanced the theory by first generalizing the one-component velocity to three-component one, and introducing a zero-expansion warp drive, suggesting that the bubble could essentially “slide” through space by changing distances along the path of motion rather than relying on volume expansion (see
Section

III.7
below).

Several theoretical analyses highlight fundamental pathologies that suggest warp drives are physically or technologically improbable: from the violation of energy conditions indicated first by
Alcubierre 1994
himself, to application of quantum inequalities by
Pfenning and Ford 1997
showing that a superluminal warp bubble would require an absurdly large amount of negative energy (which was significantly reduced in the subsequent works with further considerations)
while restricting bubble walls to nearly the Planck scale, and the “horizon” problem identified by
Krasnikov 1998
and
Everett and Roman 1997
, demonstrating that a crew cannot create or control a superluminal bubble on demand, and finally the semiclassical analysis done by
Finazzi et al. 2009
revealing a fatal instability in which Hawking radiation accumulates at the bubble’s white horizon, leading to an exponentially increasing energy density that would likely destroy the structure (and more problems which will be addressed in this work; see also
Lobo 2017
;
Lobo 2008
for more details).

Despite all these pathologies, recent works starting with
Lentz 2021a
followed by
Bobrick and Martire 2021
and
Fell and Heisenberg 2021
(and others) claim that “physically reasonable” warp-drive configurations can be constructed with positive energy density, and even more with respecting the energy conditions.
Lentz 2021b
proposes using
solitons
in an “Einstein–Maxwell-plasma” theory to achieve superluminal travel using only conventional matter sources like perfect fluid plasma. Fell and Heisenberg argue that “hidden geometric structures” in the spacetime curvature can provide a positive energy density for comoving Eulerian observers. Additionally,
Bobrick and Martire 2021
claim that specific classes of spherically symmetric or axisymmetric warp drives can satisfy the weak energy condition (WEC) or minimize its violation.

All these (and other) claims have been refuted correctly (fully or partially) by
Santiago et al. 2022
, which is one of the best work in this direction, and to which we refer the reader frequently whenever we will repeat a similar argument (we also refer the reader to this work for further references that are not cited in the present work).
3
3

3

Unfortunately, despite what has been said, this work suffers from some (minor) errors too, e.g., Errors
9
and
29
.
However, some of their correct critiques mentioned by
Santiago et al. 2022
have been ignored by the supporters of “physical” warp drive, in particular in the successive works of
Bobrick and Martire 2021
, i.e., by
Fuchs et al. 2024
,
Helmerich et al. 2024
, and by
Abellán et al. 2023a
;
Abellán et al. 2023b
,
Abellán et al. 2024
, and
Bolívar et al. 2025
, and many more.

Therefore, the present work has its objective to systematically analyze those misconceptions, misunderstandings, and mistakes which have not been observed by
Santiago et al. 2022
or any other works in the literature, and by doing so, we will also review the works mentioned above that are published after publication of
Santiago et al. 2022
, hence our work can be regarded as a complementary work to theirs. This follows in three steps that has been reflected in the title:
(i)
general formalism,
Section

II
, where we lay down the general formalism needed to study such spacetimes and list the restrictions that are imposed on such spacetimes,
(ii)
classification,
Section

III
, where we give a classification based on the metric functions and some restrictions, and finally,
(iii)
demystification,
Section

IV
, where we critically review some severe errors that appeared in the literature. Hence, this section has both negative and positive approaches in that it not only discloses errors (cf.
Section

IV
), it also reveals some geometric structures behind such spacetimes. This is done by clarifications in terms of several Remarks and new theorems, e.g., Theorems
III.15
,
IV.7
,
IV.19
,
IV.20
,
IV.32
,
IV.33
,
IV.16
,
IV.17
, but also through Errors
1
–
37
. The demystification shows that while most of the literature (in particular the recent works which claim to propose physical warp-drive spacetimes) considers the need for negative energy (i.e., violation of the WEC) and its needed amount as the main problem of the warp-drive spacetimes, there are even more fundamental issues that are not necessarily related to the violation of energy conditions. These include in particular the notions of global hyperbolicity, the asymptotic flatness, the notion of mass and energy especially the ADM energy, the Synge-G method and their consequences.

Thus, in a sense,
Section

IV
on demystification of the current warp-drive spacetimes is the heart of the present paper. Here, we take an
agonistic
approach (see
(
Synge 1971
, Chapter IV, §6)
and
Section

IV.6
below), i.e., we “wrestle” with mathematical problems and consistency—most of the time even prior to solving the field equations.
In fact, we follow a basic scientific principle (see, e.g,
Planck 1946
), which is roughly reminiscent of the logical positivism (see, e.g.,
Carnap 1934
), that we frame as follows.

Principle I.1
.

For any proposal presented within a theory, its formal (syntactic) structure must be precisely defined, and it must demonstrate consistency within that theory to prevent ungrounded speculations that could otherwise lead to the emergence of pseudo-problems
(Scheinprobleme)
.

The theory mentioned in
I.1
in this work is taken to be GR.
In the literature, there are many speculations around warp-drive spacetimes, although many of them do not satisfy
I.1
. Before revealing those inconsistencies, it may not make sense to go beyond and ponder properties of those spacetimes.
Hence, as a consequence of
I.1
, we do not consider:
(i)
quantum theoretical aspects of such spacetimes, such as quantum inequalities,
(ii)
the horizon problem (except from the discussion around the global hyperbolicity in
Section

IV.1
),
(iii)
proposals in theories beyond GR (with only exception being mentioned in
15
below).
4
4

4

For investigations of warp-drive proposals beyond general relativity, we refer the reader to, e.g.,
Carneiro et al. 2022a
;
DeBenedictis and Ilijic 2018
;
Varieschi and Burstein 2013
.

Moreover, in view of the public fascination with warp drive in popular culture and, occasionally, within the scientific literature and some space agencies, the present study restricts itself to peer-reviewed publications up to the date of submission, with emphasis on those contributions that assert the proposal of a physical warp-drive spacetime.
In fact, one can even claim that many of these works in this field violate another principle beyond
I.1
, which we may call
Thorne’s principle for a good science-fiction
that was formulated as guidelines in
(
Thorne 2014
, Section 1)
on the science behind the movie
Interstellar

202 2014
. We do not list works that violate this principle fully or partially, and leave it to the reader.

This paper is organized as follows. Before proceeding with the
Section

II
where we introduce the general formalism, we introduce the notations which we will follow throughout this work. Then, in
Section

III
we successively apply the restrictions listed there to classify the current warp-drive spacetimes. In
Section

IV
, we critically analyze misconceptions and mistakes in terms of Errors and prove some theorems.
Section

V
finally concludes the paper with some discussions and conclusions.

I.1
Notations

We denote by
\left(\mathcal{M},\bm{g}\right)
a four-dimensional Lorentzian manifold where
\bm{g}
is a metric of signature
\left(-,+,+,+\right)
.

\mathfrak{X}\left(\mathcal{N}\right)

and
{\bf T}^{p}_{q}\left(\mathcal{N}\right)
denote the vector space of all smooth vector fields
and the space of all smooth
(p,q)
-tensor fields, respectively, on any given manifold
\mathcal{N}
.
Bold letters are used for coordinate-free notation of a given
(p,q)
-tensor
{\bf A}\in{\bf T}^{p}_{q}\left(\mathcal{N}\right)
whose components are given in local coordinate bases
\{\bm{{\mathrm{d}}}x^{\alpha},\bm{\partial}_{\alpha}\}
.
Greek indices denote the spacetime coordinates, whereas Latin indices stand for spatial coordinates.
Accordingly,
\bm{\nabla}
and
\bm{D}
denote the associated covariant derivatives on
\mathcal{M}
and
\Sigma_{t}
, respectively.
The symbol
\bm{\partial}
stands for the usual Euclidean three-dimensional nabla operator.
We occasionally use the notation
\mathbf{g}(\bm{X},\bm{Y})
for the inner product of vector fields
\bm{X}
and
\bm{Y}
with respect to a given metric
\mathbf{g}
.
Therefore,
|\cdot|_{\mathbf{g}}
denotes the norm of a given tensor with respect to the metric
\mathbf{g}
, i.e., for a tensor
\bm{A}
on
\mathcal{M}
we have
|\bm{A}|_{\mathbf{g}}^{2}:=\mathrm{g}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\cdots\mathrm{g}^{\mu_{n}\nu_{n}}A_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{n}}A_{\nu_{1}\cdots\nu_{n}}
.

\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}
stands for the Laplace–Beltrami operator with respect to any metric
\mathbf{g}

and
\mathcal{L}_{\bm{X}}
denotes the Lie derivative along a given vector field
\bm{X}
.
We use the musical symbol
\flat
(for the musical isomorphism) to denote the metric-dual one-form of a given vector with respect to a given metric
\mathbf{g}
, i.e., for a generic vector field
\bm{w}\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathcal{N})
we have
{\bm{w}}^{\flat}={\mathbf{g}}\left(\bm{w},\cdot\right)
on a manifold
(\mathcal{N},\mathbf{g})
.
For any given 2-tensor fields
\bm{B}
on
(\Sigma_{t},\bm{h})
(the hypersurfaces), we denote by
{\rm I}\left[\bm{B}\right]
,
{\rm II}\left[\bm{B}\right]
,
{\rm III}\left[\bm{B}\right]
the principal scalar invariants of
\bm{B}
which can be defined as the principal scalar invariants of the matrix
\mathchoice{B^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{B^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{B^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{B^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}:=\mathchoice{\left(\bm{B}\right)^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\left(\bm{B}\right)^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\text ght)=\mathchoice{{\rm where
{\rm II}[\bm{B}]_{ij}:=\left({\rm I}[{\bm{B}}]B_{ij}-\mathchoice{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}B_{kj}\right)/2
.
The round and square brackets imply symmetrization and anti-symmetrization over the indices enclosed, respectively, and the angle brackets denote the trace-free part of a given tensor. We use units in which the speed of light is set to
c=1
.

II
General formalism and restrictions

In this section we outline the general
3+1
formulation, thoroughly discussed in
Buchert et al. 2020
;
Gourgoulhon 2012
;
Alcubierre 2008
;
Smarr and York 1978
;
Baumgarte and Shapiro 2010
and
Barzegar and Buchert 2025
; we essentially summarize the needed formalism and formulas from
Buchert et al. 2020
(see also
Barzegar and Buchert 2025
), and to maintain brevity, we refer the
reader to
Ehlers 1993
;
Gourgoulhon 2012
;
Alcubierre 2008
;
Smarr and York 1978
;
Wald 1984
for further details. We refer to these references sparingly to avoid repetition throughout this section.

II.1
Foliations, metric and the geometry of motion

We consider a four-dimensional Lorentzian manifold
\left(\mathcal{M},\bm{g}\right)
, where
\bm{g}=g_{\mu\nu}\,{\mathbf{d}}x^{\mu}\otimes{\mathbf{d}}x^{\nu}
in local coordinates
\left(x^{\mu}\right)=\left(t,x^{i}\right)\equiv\left(t,\bm{x}\right)
with its Levi-Civita connection
\bm{\nabla}
, and
\mathcal{M}\cong\Sigma_{t}\times I
, where
\Sigma_{t}
, with a global time function
t\in I\subset\mathbb{R}
, is a Riemannian
3
-manifold. In other words,
\Sigma_{t}
denotes a family of spacelike hypersurfaces that foliate the spacetime
\mathcal{M}
.

The matter model considered in this work is a single general fluid with the
4
-velocity
\bm{u}
that describes the unit, future-oriented, timelike tangent
4
-vector field of a timelike congruence, i.e., the fluid flow.
The unit, timelike, future-oriented normal vector field of
\Sigma_{t}
is denoted by
\bm{n}
that satisfies
{\bm{g}}\left(\bm{n},\bm{n}\right)=-1
. Observers with the
4
-velocity
\bm{n}
are called
Eulerian
.

Using
\bm{n}
, we construct the bilinear form
\bm{h}=h_{\alpha\beta}\,\mathbf{d}x^{\alpha}\otimes\mathbf{d}x^{\beta}
, and the projector
\mathchoice{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}
,
which projects spacetime tensors onto the hypersurfaces of the foliation with the following form and properties:

\displaystyle h_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=g_{\mu\nu}+n_{\mu}n_{\nu}\,;

(2.1)

defines the induced Riemannian metric (the first fundamental form)

for a given tensor field
\bm{F}\in{\bf T}^{p}_{q}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
.

Another important vector field is the so-called
time vector

\bm{\partial}_{t}
, whose integral curve keeps the same spatial coordinates that it attains on the initial hypersurface satisfying,
{\bm{g}}(\bm{\partial}_{t},\bm{\nabla}t)=1
. The time vector represents the flow of time throughout the spacetime and can be decomposed into vectors normal and tangent to
\Sigma_{t}
as

\bm{\partial}_{t}=N\bm{n}+\bm{N}\,,

(2.2)

where
N
and
\bm{N}
are the
lapse function
and the
shift vector
, respectively defined by
N:=-{\bm{g}}(\bm{n},\bm{\partial}_{t})
and
N_{i}:=h_{ij}(\bm{\partial}_{t})^{j}
with
{\bm{g}}(\bm{n},\bm{N})=0
. The time vector can,
a priori
, be null, space- or time-like, and in general it is not normalized. A natural class of observers are the ones associated to the time vector
\bm{\partial}_{t}
whenever it is timelike, which we call the
coordinate observers

Barzegar and Buchert 2025
,
5
5

5

Coordinate observers are sometimes referred to as
static observers
in the static (or stationary) context.
by normalizing the time vector
\bm{\partial}_{t}
having the
4
-velocity
6
6

6

An ambiguity appeared in
(
Barzegar and Buchert 2025
, Equation (11))
: the timelike character of such observers is assumed there without being explicit, in which case the absolute value enclosing the prefactor of
\bm{\partial}_{t}
is not necessary.

\hat{\bm{\partial}}_{t}:=\left(N^{2}-N^{k}N_{k}\right)^{-1/2}\bm{\partial}_{t}\,.

(2.3)

Now, we can express the unit normal vector
\bm{n}
and its metric dual form
{\bm{n}}^{\flat}
with respect to coordinate basis
\{\bm{\partial}_{\mu}\}
using (
2.2
) as

\bm{n}=\frac{1}{N}\left(1,-\bm{N}\right)\,,\quad{\bm{n}}^{\flat}=-N\,(1,\bm{0})\,,

(2.4)

whose
4
-acceleration is given by (cf., e.g.,
Gourgoulhon 2012
;
Smarr and York 1978
;
Buchert et al. 2020
)

\mathfrak{a}^{\mu}:=n^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}n^{\mu}=h^{\mu\nu}D_{\nu}\ln N\,.

(2.5)

Finally, using all relations above, one can decompose the four-dimensional line element into the Arnowitt–Deser–Misner (ADM) form (cf.
Arnowitt et al. 2008a
),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}s^{2}

\displaystyle=g_{\alpha\beta}\,{\mathrm{d}}x^{\alpha}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\beta}

(2.6)

\displaystyle=-\left(N^{2}-N^{k}N_{k}\right){\mathrm{d}}t^{2}+2N_{i}\,{\mathrm{d}}x^{i}\,{\mathrm{d}}t\,+h_{ij}\,{\mathrm{d}}x^{i}{\mathrm{d}}x^{j}\,,

where
N^{i}=h^{ij}N_{j}
.

Moreover, the second fundamental form (or the extrinsic curvature)
{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}=\mathcal{K}_{{\mu\nu}}\,{\mathbf{d}}x^{\mu}\otimes{\mathbf{d}}x^{\nu}
is defined by

{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\left(\bm{W},\bm{Z}\right):=\bm{g}\left(\bm{n},\bm{\nabla}_{\bm{W}}\bm{Z}\right)=-\bm{g}\left(\bm{\nabla}_{\bm{W}}\bm{n},\bm{Z}\right)
, for all
\bm{W},\bm{Z}\in\mathfrak{X}\left(\Sigma_{t}\right)

which in components reads

\mathcal{K}_{{\mu\nu}}=-\mathchoice{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{h^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{h^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{h^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{h^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}\nabla_{\alpha}n_{\beta}=-\nabla_{\mu}n_{\nu}-n_{\mu}D_{\nu}\ln N\,,

(2.7)

whose trace is the mean curvature
\mathcal{K}:=\tr_{\bm{h}}{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}=h^{ij}\mathcal{K}_{ij}=-\nabla_{\mu}n^{\mu}
.

The fluid can, in general, have vorticity. Hence, the spacetime
cannot
, by the Frobenius theorem, be foliated in such a way that the unit normal vector is the
4
-velocity of the fluid, which is the general assumption for what we call “restricted warp drives” (see
Barzegar and Buchert 2025
).

Now, we consider a general fluid with the
4
-velocity
\bm{u}
that is, in general, tilted with respect to the unit normal vector field
\bm{n}
, which can be decomposed as follows (cf., e.g.,
Buchert et al. 2020
;
Gourgoulhon 2012
;
Wainwright and Ellis 1997
;
Barzegar and Buchert 2025
):

{\bm{u}}=\gamma({\bm{v}})\left(\bm{n}+\bm{v}\right)\,,

(2.8)

with
n^{\mu}v_{\mu}=0
and

\gamma({\bm{v}}):=-n^{\mu}u_{\mu}=\left(1-v^{\mu}v_{\mu}\right)^{-1/2}\,,

(2.9)

where
\bm{v}=v^{i}\bm{\partial}_{i}

is the
covariant spatial velocity
field. It is a measure of tilt between the unit normal and fluid frame that is equal to the
covariant Lorentz factor

\gamma({\bm{v}})
(cf.
Barzegar and Buchert 2025
).
In the
flow-orthogonal
case, where
\bm{u}=\bm{n}
, we have
\bm{v}=\bm{0}
and
\gamma(\bm{0})=1
, hence no tilt.

Another important vector is the spatial fluid’s coordinate velocity field (referred to as
coordinate velocity
) defined by

\bm{V}:=\frac{{\mathrm{d}}\bm{x}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\,,\quad\mathrm{with}\quad n_{\mu}V^{\mu}=0\,,

(2.10)

where
{\mathrm{d}}\bm{x}
is the displacement of the spatial position of a fluid element in the coordinate system
(t,\bm{x})
, hence it is (spatially) coordinate dependent, in contrast to the covariant spatial velocity. The coordinate velocity
\bm{V}
should not be confused with the covariant velocity
\bm{v}
introduced in (
2.8
) which can be written as (cf.
Barzegar and Buchert 2025
)
7
7

7

The reader must take care about the notation as
v^{i}
or
v
is usually used in the literature for the coordinate velocity in this context.

\bm{v}=\frac{1}{N}\left(\bm{N}+\bm{V}\right)\,.

(2.11)

Hence,

\bm{u}=\frac{\gamma({\bm{v}})}{N}\left(N\bm{n}+\bm{N}+\bm{V}\right)\,,

(2.12)

where

\frac{\gamma({\bm{v}})}{N}=\left(N^{2}-\left|\bm{N}+\bm{V}\right|_{\bm{h}}^{2}\right)^{-1/2}\,.

(2.13)

From (
2.12
) we obtain the component expressions for
\bm{u}
and
{\bm{u}}^{\flat}
:

\displaystyle\bm{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma({\bm{v}})}{N}\left(1,\bm{V}\right)\,,

(2.14)

\displaystyle{\bm{u}}^{\flat}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma({\bm{v}})}{N}\left(-N^{2}+N^{i}\left(N_{i}+V_{i}\right),\,{\bm{N}}^{\flat}+{\bm{V}}^{\flat}\right)\,,

where, as mentioned in
Section

I.1
, we used the notation
{\bm{X}}^{\flat}:={\bm{h}}\left(\bm{X},\cdot\right)
for all vector fields
\bm{X}\in\mathfrak{X}\left(\Sigma_{t}\right)
. It is easy to show that

V^{i}=\frac{u^{i}}{u^{0}}\,.

(2.15)

One can now specify different coordinate systems, e.g.,

(
i
)

for a coordinate system comoving with the fluid, which corresponds to a specific shift, we have

\bm{V}=\bm{0}
,

(
ii
)

for a vanishing tilt we have

\bm{V}=-\bm{N}

(2.16)

which results from (
2.11
), independent of the choice of the shift vector.

Choosing
\bm{n}=\bm{u}
(hence
\bm{v}=\bm{0}
and
\gamma({\bm{0}})=1
)
corresponds to a foliation orthogonal to the fluid flow which is only possible if the fluid flow has no vorticity.
Even for irrotational fluids, introducing a tilt allows us
to keep the freedom in the construction of the spatial hypersurfaces.

Remark II.1
.

In the adapted coordinates introduced above, for any vector field
\bm{X}\in\mathfrak{X}(\Sigma_{t})
, we have
X^{0}=0
which follows from
n_{\mu}X^{\mu}=0
and
n_{i}=0
. However, from
n^{\mu}X_{\mu}=0
we have
X_{0}=N^{i}X_{i}=N_{i}X^{i}
(cf., e.g.,
Buchert et al. 2020
). The same applies to other spatial tensors that are orthogonal to
\bm{n}
.

II.2
Kinematics and dynamics of the fluid

In the tilted situation, we introduce the bilinear form
\bm{b}=b_{\alpha\beta}\,\mathbf{d}x^{\alpha}\otimes\mathbf{d}x^{\beta}
with

\displaystyle b_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=g_{\mu\nu}+u_{\mu}u_{\nu}\,;

(2.17)

\displaystyle b_{\alpha\mu}u^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=0\,,\;\;\quad\mathchoice{b^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{b^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{b^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{b^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}\mathchoice{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyl \scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{b^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}\,,\;\;\quad b^{\alpha\beta}b_{\alpha\beta}=3\,,

where
\mathchoice{b^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{b^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{b^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{b^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}
allows us to project tensors onto the local rest frames of the fluid (orthogonal to
\bm{u}
).
Note that the presence of a tilt between
\bm{u}
and
\bm{n}
results in two different projectors
\bm{b}
and
\bm{h}
. Using (
2.17
), we decompose the
4
-covariant derivative of the
1
-form
{\bm{u}}^{\flat}
(cf.
Ehlers 1993
;
Buchert et al. 2020
):
8
8

8

See
Footnote

11
for a convention regarding the indices.

\nabla_{\nu}u_{\mu}=-a_{\mu}\,u_{\nu}+\theta_{\mu\nu}+\omega_{\mu\nu}\,,

(2.18)

with

\displaystyle a_{\mu}

\displaystyle:=u^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}u_{\mu}\,,

(2.19a)

\displaystyle\theta_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=\mathchoice{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{b^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{b^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{b^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{b^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}\nabla_{(\alpha}u_{\beta)}=:\sigma_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{3}\theta b_{\mu\nu}\,,

(2.19b)

\displaystyle\theta

\displaystyle:=\tr_{\bm{g}}\bm{\theta}=\nabla_{\alpha}u^{\alpha}\,,

(2.19c)

\displaystyle\omega_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=\mathchoice{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{b^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{b^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{b^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{b^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}\nabla_{[\beta}u_{\alpha]}\,,

(2.19d)

where
a^{\mu}
are the components of the 4-acceleration
\bm{a}
of the fluid,
\theta_{\mu\nu}
and
\theta
the components of the fluid’s expansion tensor and expansion rate of
\bm{\theta}
,
\sigma_{\mu\nu}
the components of the fluid’s shear tensor
\bm{\sigma}
, and
\omega_{\mu\nu}
the components of the fluid’s vorticity tensor
\bm{\omega}
. All these quantities are orthogonal to
\bm{u}
:

u^{\mu}a_{\mu}=0\,,\quad u^{\mu}\theta_{\mu\nu}=0\,,\quad u^{\mu}\omega_{\mu\nu}=0\,.

(2.20)

Note that the rest frames of the fluid are not hypersurface-forming if
\bm{\omega}
does not vanish due to Frobenius’s theorem. Finally, one can define the squared
rate of shear
and
rate of vorticity
,

\sigma^{2}:=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\,\sigma^{\mu\nu}\,,\quad\omega^{2}:=\frac{1}{2}\omega_{\mu\nu}\,\omega^{\mu\nu}\,,

(2.21)

which are both nonnegative. Moreover, from the anti-symmetrizing (
2.18
) it follows

\omega_{\mu\nu}=a_{[\mu}u_{\nu]}-\nabla_{[\mu}u_{\nu]}=a_{[\mu}u_{\nu]}-\partial_{[\mu}u_{\nu]}\,.

(2.22)

II.3
The matter model and energy-momentum conservation equations

The matter model we consider in this work is a single general fluid with the energy-momentum tensor
\bm{T}=T_{\mu\nu}\,{\mathbf{d}}x^{\mu}\otimes{\mathbf{d}}x^{\nu}
that can be written with respect to the fluid rest frames with the induced metric
\bm{b}
as

T_{\mu\nu}=\epsilon\,u_{\mu}u_{\nu}+2\,q_{(\mu}u_{\nu)}+p_{\mu\nu}\,,

(2.23)

where

\displaystyle\epsilon

\displaystyle:=u^{\alpha}u^{\beta}T_{\alpha\beta}\,,

(2.24a)

\displaystyle q_{\mu}

\displaystyle:=-\mathchoice{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}u^{\beta}T_{\alpha\beta}\,,

(2.24b)

\displaystyle p_{{\mu\nu}}

\displaystyle:=\mathchoice{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{b^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{b^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[ (2.24c)

with the properties

u^{\mu}q_{\mu}=0\quad,\quad u^{\mu}\pi_{\mu\nu}=0\quad,\quad b^{\mu\nu}\pi_{\mu\nu}=0\ ,

(2.25)

and where
\epsilon
denotes the energy density of the fluid in its rest frames,
q^{\mu}
the components of the momentum density
\bm{q}
(energy flux relative to
\bm{u}
),
p_{\mu\nu}
its stress density,

p:=b^{{\mu\nu}}p_{\mu\nu}/3
the isotropic pressure, and
\pi_{\mu\nu}
the components of the traceless anisotropic stress
\bm{\pi}
.

Alternatively, the energy-momentum tensor can be decomposed with respect to the normal frames with the induced metric
\bm{h}
as

T_{\mu\nu}=E\,n_{\mu}n_{\nu}+2\,J_{(\mu}n_{\nu)}+S_{\mu\nu}\,,

(2.26)

with

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle:=n^{\alpha}n^{\beta}T_{\alpha\beta}\,,

(2.27a)

\displaystyle J_{\mu}

\displaystyle:=-h^{\alpha}_{\phantom{\alpha}\mu}n^{\beta}T_{\alpha\beta}\,,

(2.27b)

\displaystyle S_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=h^{\alpha}_{\phantom{\alpha}\mu}h^{\beta}_{\phantom{\beta}\nu}T_{\alpha\beta}=:\Pi_{{\mu\nu}}+Ph_{{\mu\nu}}\,,

(2.27c)

where
E
is the energy density of the fluid,
J_{\mu}
its momentum density,
S_{\mu\nu}
its stress density,
P:=h^{{\mu\nu}}S_{\mu\nu}/3
the isotropic pressure, and
\Pi_{\mu\nu}
the components of the spatial and traceless anisotropic stress, all as measured in the normal frames (by Eulerian observers).
Moreover, the following relations hold:

(2.28)

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle:=g^{{\alpha\beta}}T_{\alpha\beta}=-E+3P\,.

), after some lengthy but straightforward calculations, we can relate Eulerian rest frame and fluid rest frame variables as follows:

For the application to warp-drive spacetimes, these general extrinsic relations will become relevant for an Eulerian observer looking at the moving spaceship in the tilted situation.

\displaystyle h_{\mu\nu}-n_{\mu}n_{\nu}

\displaystyle=b_{\mu\nu}-u_{\mu}u_{\nu}\,,

(2.30a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}E&=\gamma^{2}(\epsilon+p)-p+2\,\gamma v^{\alpha}q_{\alpha}\\
&\quad+\pi_{\alpha\beta}v^{\alpha}v^{\beta}\,,\end{split}

(2.30b)

\displaystyle E-3P

\displaystyle=\epsilon-3p\,,

(2.30c)

\displaystyle\begin{split}J_{\mu}&=\gamma q_{\mu}+\gamma^{2}\left(\epsilon+p+\gamma^{-1}q^{\alpha}v_{\alpha}\right)v_{\mu}\\
&\quad+v^{\alpha}\pi_{\mu\alpha}\\
&\quad-\left(\gamma v^{\alpha}q_{\alpha}+\pi_{\alpha\beta}v^{\alpha}v^{\beta}\right)n_{\mu}\,,\end{split}

(2.30d)

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{\mu\nu}&=ph_{\mu\nu}+\pi_{\mu\nu}+\gamma^{2}\left(\epsilon+p\right)v_{\mu}v_{\nu}\\
&\quad+\pi_{\alpha\beta}v^{\alpha}v^{\beta}n_{\mu}n_{\nu}-2v^{\alpha}\pi_{\alpha(\mu}n_{\nu)}\\
&\quad+2\gamma\left[q_{(\mu}v_{\nu)}-v^{\alpha}q_{\alpha}n_{(\mu}v_{\nu)}\right]\,.\end{split}

(2.30e)

Remark II.2
.

Using the fact that
n_{i}=0
, we expressed many terms on the right-hand sides of (
2.30
) in terms of
n_{\mu}
so that the spatial components of the Eulerian quantities will simplify.

Remark II.3
.

For a perfect fluid, the relations in (
2.30
) reduce to

\displaystyle h_{\mu\nu}-n_{\mu}n_{\nu}

\displaystyle=b_{\mu\nu}-u_{\mu}u_{\nu}\,,

(2.31a)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}(\epsilon+p)-p\,,

(2.31b)

\displaystyle E-3P

\displaystyle=\epsilon-3p\,,

(2.31c)

\displaystyle J_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}\left(\epsilon+p\right)v_{\mu}\,,

(2.31d)

\displaystyle S_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=ph_{\mu\nu}+\gamma^{2}\left(\epsilon+p\right)v_{\mu}v_{\nu}\,.

(2.31e)

Evidently, if
\bm{u}=\bm{n}
, then all quantities introduced above coincide. In this work, we will write the field equations in terms of the Eulerian quantities, since this is the
creative
approach taken in the literature; otherwise, a
realistic
approach should be adopted where the matter quantities should be used (see
(
Synge 1971
, Chapter IV, §6)
and
Section

IV.6
below).
We finish this part by a lemma which relates
\theta_{\mu\nu}
to
\mathcal{K}_{\mu\nu}
.

Lemma II.4
.

The expansion tensor and the second fundamental form in the tilted case are related to each other by the following equation:

\displaystyle\theta_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-\gamma\mathcal{K}_{\mu\nu}+\gamma\nabla_{(\mu}v_{\nu)}+\gamma^{-1}u_{(\mu}\partial_{\nu)}\gamma

(2.32)

\displaystyle+a_{(\mu}u_{\nu)}-\gamma\mathfrak{a}_{(\mu}n_{\nu)}\,,

with
\mathfrak{a}_{\mu}
from (
2.5
).

II.3.1
Conservation of energy and momentum in fluid rest frames

From
{\nabla}_{\beta}T^{\alpha\beta}=0
and using the notation
\dot{\bm{F}}:=\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{F}
for any given tensor field
\bm{F}
, we deduce the energy-momentum conservation laws as measured in fluid rest frames (cf.
Buchert et al. 2020
), by contracting along
u_{\alpha}
:

\dot{\epsilon}+\theta\left(\epsilon+p\right)+a_{\alpha}q^{\alpha}+\nabla_{\alpha}q^{\alpha}+\pi^{\alpha\beta}\sigma_{\alpha\beta}=0\,,

(2.33)

and by contracting with
b_{\mu\alpha}
:

\displaystyle(\epsilon+p)a_{\mu}+\mathchoice{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{b^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\nabla_{\alpha}p+b_{\mu\alpha}\dot{q}^{\alpha}

(2.34)

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{3}\theta q_{\mu}+q^{\alpha}(\sigma_{\mu\alpha}+\omega_{\mu\alpha})+b_{\mu\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\pi^{\alpha\beta}=0\,.

II.3.2
Conservation of energy and momentum in Eulerian rest frames

We now derive the energy-momentum conservation laws as measured in Eulerian rest frames. From (
2.26
) and
{\nabla}_{\beta}T^{\alpha\beta}=0
, we get (cf.
(
Gourgoulhon 2012
, Equation (6.2))
):

\displaystyle\left(n^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}E-EK\right)n_{\alpha}+ED_{\alpha}\ln N-J^{\mu}\mathcal{K}_{\mu\alpha}

(2.35)

\displaystyle+n_{\alpha}\nabla_{\mu}J^{\mu}+n^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}J_{\alpha}-\mathcal{K}J_{\alpha}+\nabla_{\mu}\mathchoice{S^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{S^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{S^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{S^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}=0\,.

Similar to the previous subsection, contracting the above with
n^{\alpha}
and
\mathchoice{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}
, we arrive at the energy-momentum conservation laws as measured in Eulerian rest frames (cf.
(
Gourgoulhon 2012
, Equations (6.10) and (6.20))
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{N}(\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}_{\bm{N}})E&+D_{i}J^{i}+2J^{i}D_{i}\ln N\\
&\quad-\mathcal{K}_{ij}S^{ij}-E\mathcal{K}=0\,,\end{split}

(2.36)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{N}(\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}_{\bm{N}})J_{\alpha}&+D_{\mu}\mathchoice{S^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{S^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{S^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{S^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}+\mathchoice{S^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{S^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{S^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{S^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}D_{\mu}\ln N\\
&\quad-\mathcal{K}J_{\alpha}+ED_{\alpha}\ln N=0\,,\end{split}

(2.37)

where we used the fact that
\mathchoice{h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}n^{\nu}\nabla_{\nu}J_{\mu}-J^{\mu}\mathcal{K}_{\mu\alpha}=N^{-1}(\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}_{\bm{N}})J_{\alpha}
(cf.
(
Gourgoulhon 2012
, Section 6.2.4)
).
Note that

\mathcal{L}_{\bm{n}}\Phi=N^{-1}(\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}_{\bm{N}})\Phi\,;\quad\forall\,\Phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})\,.

(2.38)

II.3.3
Rest mass, its density and conservation

Finally, we define another important local quantity that satisfies a conservation law which is motivated by the conservation of (baryonic) particle number (see, e.g.,
Gourgoulhon 2012
;
Baumgarte and Shapiro 2010
;
Buchert et al. 2020
). To an (isolated) system that contains matter we can attribute a conserved
rest mass
; indeed, since we presumed the existence of a fundamental
4
-velocity of a fluid, we can define its
mass current density

\bm{m}
as

\bm{m}:=\varrho\bm{u}\,,

(2.39)

which satisfies the continuity equation

\nabla_{\alpha}m^{\alpha}=\nabla_{\alpha}(\varrho u^{\alpha})=0\,,

(2.40)

which is equivalent to

u^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}\varrho+\theta\varrho=0\,.

(2.41)

The
rest mass density

\varrho
of the fluid in its rest frames is therefore defined as such, out of which one can determine the rest mass.
To this end, we consider the rest mass density as measured by the Eulerian observer given by

\mathscr{N}:=-m^{\alpha}n_{\alpha}\,,

(2.42)

which is related to the (baryon) number density.
The above combined with (
2.9
) yields

\mathscr{N}=\gamma\varrho\,.

(2.43)

Now, integrating (
2.40
) over a “pilled-boxed-shaped” spacetime region bounded by a spatial domain
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
connected by a timelike mantle surface (see
(
Baumgarte and Shapiro 2010
, Section 3.5)
), and using the Gauß theorem, one can show that the rest mass
M_{\mathcal{D}}
over a spatial region
\mathcal{D}
containing the matter source, i.e.,

M_{\mathcal{D}}:=\int_{\mathcal{D}}\gamma\varrho\sqrt{\det\bm{h}}\,{\mathrm{d}}^{3}x=\int_{\mathcal{D}}Nu^{0}\sqrt{\det\bm{h}}\,{\mathrm{d}}^{3}x

(2.44)

is conserved.

Analogously, the mass current density measured by the Eulerian observer can be defined by projecting
\bm{m}
onto the spatial hypersurfaces as

M^{\alpha}:=\mathchoice{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}m^{\beta}=\gamma\varrho v^{\alpha}=\mathcal{P}v^{\alpha}\,.

(2.45)

Using the relations above, the continuity equation (
2.40
) can be written in terms of quantities measured by the Eulerian observers as (cf.
(
Gourgoulhon 2012
, Section 6.3.2)
)

\left(\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}_{\bm{N}}\right)\mathcal{P}-N\mathcal{K}\mathcal{P}+D_{i}\left(N\mathcal{P}v^{i}\right)=0\,,

(2.46)

where we used (
2.8
) and

\nabla_{\alpha}X^{\alpha}=D_{i}X^{i}+X^{i}D_{i}\ln N\,;\,\,\forall\,\,\bm{X}\in\mathfrak{X}(\Sigma_{t})\,.

(2.47)

II.4
The 3+1 Einstein equations

In this subsection, we briefly introduce the
3+1
form of the Einstein equations

\bm{G}+\mathrm{\Lambda}\,\bm{g}=8\pi G\,\bm{T}\,,

(2.48)

where

\bm{G}:=\textbf{Ric}-\frac{1}{2}{\rm R}\,\bm{g}

(2.49)

is the Einstein tensor, with
Ric
being the Ricci curvature tensor of the spacetime,
{\rm R}:=\tr_{\bm{g}}\textbf{Ric}
, and
\mathrm{\Lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
is the cosmological constant. The evolution equations are (cf., e.g.,
Arnowitt et al. 2008a
;
Gourgoulhon 2012
;
Alcubierre 2008
;
Smarr and York 1978
;
Buchert et al. 2020
)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}h_{ij}

\displaystyle=-2\left[N\mathcal{K}_{ij}-D_{(i}N_{j)}\right]\,,

(2.50)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}

\displaystyle=N\Big\{\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}+\mathcal{K}\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.7135 G\big[\left(3P-E\right)\mathchoice{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}-2\mathchoice{S^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{S^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c] \displaystyle\qquad\quad-\mathrm{\Lambda}\mathchoice{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}\Big\}-D_{j}D^{i}N
