Error 23
.

Abellán et al. 2024
make another fundamental mistake: for their proposed model, they show that the NEC and the WEC are violated, but the DEC is not (see
Figure

1
)! This clearly points at a deep flaw not only in their analysis and numerics (
IV.27
), but also in their conception (cf.
21
).
24
24

24

Even setting aside this fundamental issue (and other more important ones mentioned in the present work), the main point of
Abellán et al. 2024
remains unclear, as their model suffers from the violation of all energy conditions.

Sometimes (pointwise) energy conditions are investigated in a wrong way where either they have no meaning, or they are not valid. We recognize at least two instances of such cases, which we formulate in Remarks
IV.29
and
IV.30
and bring the examples in Errors
24
and
25
, respectively.

Remark IV.29
.

If the spacetime metric is below
C^{2}
, i.e., twice continuously differentiable, then the “classical” energy conditions lose their meaning as the Einstein tensor
\bm{G}
, hence the energy-momentum tensor
\bm{T}=(8\pi G)^{-1}\bm{G}
are not well-defined in this case. In such cases one needs a distributional version of the classical energy conditions (see
(
Steinbauer 2023
, Section 4.4)
for an excellent example in the context of singularity theorems for metrics of low regularity). See
24
, where this fact is neglected.

Error 24
.

The metric of the model proposed by
Lentz 2021a
(and at least the first example by
Fell and Heisenberg 2021
) is clearly below
C^{2}
. Therefore, even if the proposal by
Lentz 2021a
was physically sound (which is certainly not the case), by
IV.29
the classical energy conditions have no meaning in this situation.

Remark IV.30
.

If a thin shell (or other similar configuration) is considered, then the energy conditions should be evaluated for the induced energy-momentum tensor on the junction surface (cf., e.g.,
Goldwirth and Katz 1995
, and for a recent account of this topic
Maeda 2023
). See
25
, where this fact is ignored.

Error 25
.

Bolívar et al. 2025
consider a thin shell in a static setting,
25
25

25

It is not clear what the point of this setting is, as it is not the goal of a warp drive, see
11
.
and investigate the (pointwise) energy conditions of the energy-momentum tensor resulting from the Einstein tensor, thereby neglecting the fact that the induced energy-momentum tensor on the junction surface should be analyzed, see
IV.30
.

After this general consideration, we start with the WEC since almost all works focus primarily on it due to its most intuitive character.

IV.8.1
Weak energy condition

Energy conditions are, in general, conditions required to ensure that “energy should be positive”
Curiel 2017
. Clearly, the simplest and the most intuitive energy condition that reflects this requirement is the
WEC
. Therefore, the violation of the WEC is equivalent to the existence of negative energy and mass. Accordingly, the majority of works in the field of warp-drive spacetimes are focused on the validity of the
WEC
, particularly those that aim to resolve its violation.

Before turning to Errors, we gather some general results in terms of theorems. We start by recalling that
R
-Warp models generically violate either the
WEC
or the
SEC
.

Theorem IV.31

(Natário, 2002)
.

R
-Warp models violate either the WEC or the SEC.

Proof.

See
Natário 2002
for the proof.
∎

Theorem IV.32
.

The Natário zero-expansion warp drive violates the WEC.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{K}={\rm I}[\bm{\mathcal{K}}]=0
, then
{\rm II}[\bm{\mathcal{K}}]=-\left|\bm{\mathcal{K}}\right|_{\bm{h}}^{2}/2\leq 0
, which shows the violation of the WEC by the Hamilton constraint (
3.11
), unless
{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\equiv\bm{0}
identically, which yields exactly Minkowski spacetime that can be easily concluded from (
3.33
) together with (
2.53
).
∎

In fact,
Theorem

IV.32
is a special corollary of the following theorem.

Theorem IV.33
.

If a non-vacuum spacetime admits a vanishing mean-curvature foliation whose Ricci scalar satisfies
\mathcal{R}<2\mathrm{\Lambda}+\left|\bm{\mathcal{K}}\right|_{\bm{h}}^{2}
, then the WEC is violated.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{K}=0
, then the Hamilton constraint (
2.62
) implies that
E<0
, when
\mathcal{R}<2\mathrm{\Lambda}+\left|\bm{\mathcal{K}}\right|_{\bm{h}}^{2}
. If
\mathcal{R}=0=\mathrm{\Lambda}
, one recovers
Theorem

IV.32
for
N=1
.
∎

Error 26
.

Sometimes the fact that most of
R
-Warp models, in particular Alcubierre warp drive, violate the WEC is associated to the superluminal motion. However, as it is clear from (
3.48
), this fact is independent of the value of
V_{s}
, and it is rather a property of such spacetimes. For reasons of brevity, we do not list the works that made this mistake.

Error 27
.

Bobrick and Martire 2021
claim that the fact that the WEC is violated in the Alcubierre and Natário spacetimes is due to the truncation of the warp field and spherical symmetry of these spacetimes. As mentioned in Errors
4
and
10
, both the latter statements and the conclusion are flawed. This idea was repeated uncritically by
Fell and Heisenberg 2021
, as it is already pointed out correctly by
Santiago et al. 2022
.

As explained and criticized duly by
Santiago et al. 2022
, positivity of the Eulerian energy (
2.27a
) does not guarantee that the WEC is satisfied, since by definition
WEC
is satisfied only when the inequality holds for all timelike vectors.

Error 28
.

In many works,
26
26

26

Santiago et al. 2022
rightly raised this point concerning several of the works listed here. We mention them again, but we add to them the instances which were not published at the time.

including those by
Bobrick and Martire 2021
;
Fell and Heisenberg 2021
;
Lentz 2021a
;
Garattini and Zatrimaylov 2024
,
Abellán et al. 2023b
;
Abellán et al. 2023a
(see
Santiago et al. 2022
for more critiques on works on which
Abellán et al. 2023b
;
Abellán et al. 2023a
are based) take the WEC to be equivalent to the positivity of the Eulerian energy (
2.27a
), without proving that the WEC is in fact satisfied.

Error 29
.

Sometimes
E_{\text{tot}}
from (
4.1
) is used to speculate why the WEC is violated. For example,

Santiago et al. 2022
argue that the WEC is violated since
E_{\text{tot}}=0
should hold. But, as we mentioned in
Section

IV.3
(see also
IV.24
),
E_{\text{tot}}
is not related to the ADM mass, in general. Moreover, as mentioned in
7
, the given falloff for the metric components is not enough to conclude the asymptotic flatness.
This error is therefore a combination of two mistakes regarding
IV.13
and the notion of energy.

Error 30
.

Lentz 2021b
attempts to argue that the argument by
Santiago et al. 2022
illustrated in
29
(which is indeed incorrect) cannot be applied to the model proposed by
Lentz 2021a
, because the Eulerian energy is not smooth, hence one cannot apply Stokes’ theorem as usual. This argument suffers from many mistakes:
(i)
the Eulerian energy therein is not continuous, it is at most piecewise continuous or differentiable,
(ii)
even if the arguing with
E_{\text{tot}}
given in (
4.1
) was in order, the negative term is absent in this model since the coordinate vorticity vanishes,
(iii)
and finally, even if the Eulerian energy in that sense is positive, this, by
IV.29
, has nothing to do with the validity of the classical WEC, since it is not well-defined (even assuming its validity, the author does not show it). Lastly, even if one neglects all these problems, and assumes that the derivation of equations are correct, as mentioned in
28
,
Lentz 2021a
offers no proof for the claim that the WEC is not violated.

IV.8.2
Null energy condition

Although the violation of the NEC is rather trivial for simple
R
-Warp models, such as the Alcubierre and the zero-expansion Natário warp drives (see
Alcubierre and Lobo 2017
;
Santiago et al. 2022
), a direct proof for the violation of the NEC for
R
-Warp models was given by
Santiago et al. 2022
only recently.

Theorem IV.34

(
Santiago et al. 2022
)
.

R
-Warp models violate the NEC.

Remark IV.35
.

III.1
, i.e., the characteristic property of the warp bubble together with
R4
, play a fundamental role in proving
Theorem

IV.34
. Indeed, a generic metric of the form (
3.26
) does
not
, in general, violate the WEC, let alone the NEC if these two assumptions, especially
III.1
are not imposed, i.e., if the metric is not supposed to describe a warp-drive spacetime.
An example of this kind was observed by
Shoshany and Snodgrass 2024
where
E=0=\Omega_{ij}
, in which case the violation of the WEC cannot be concluded without further considerations.

It should be noted that using global techniques and a definition for superluminal travel that requires a path, in a specific sense, to be faster than all neighboring ones, and relies on the assumption of the
generic condition
(cf.
(
Hawking and Ellis 2023
, Section 4.4)
for a definition, see also
(
Shoshany and Snodgrass 2024
, Section 4.2)
for the related discussion),
Olum 1998
shows the violation of the NEC, although the result is stated as the violation of the WEC. Of course, by the hierarchy of the energy conditions (see,
Figure

1
), if the NEC is violated, then all the other energy conditions, in particular, the WEC is violated. Nonetheless,
Olum 1998
actually showed the violation of the NEC, and not merely the violation of the WEC, as reported throughout the literature, with only a few exceptions.

However, as shown by
Shoshany and Snodgrass 2024
, none of the conditions mentioned above is satisfied for a wide variety of
R
-Warp models that have a shape function similar to (
3.45
), e.g., the Alcubierre and Van Den Broeck warp drives introduced in
Section

III.8
and
Section

III.5.1
, respectively. Thus, one
cannot
apply the proof by
Olum 1998
to the
R
-Warp models.

Remark IV.36
.

As explained in
6
, the example given by
Schuster et al. 2023
does not provide an example for violation of “generic” Natário warp-drive spacetimes.

IV.8.3
Dominant energy condition

In the notation used in
Section

II.3
, the
DEC
is satisfied iff (see, e.g.,
Schoen and Yau 1981
,
Witten 1981
,
(
Gourgoulhon 2012
, Section 8.3.4)
, and
(
Straumann 2013
, Section 3.7.3)
)

E\geq\left|\bm{J}\right|_{\bm{h}}\quad\text{and}\quad E\geq 0\,,

(4.8)

where
\bm{J}=J^{i}\bm{\partial}_{i}
(see also
IV.28
).

We start with the following trivial corollary to
Theorem

IV.20
, which says that
R
-Warp models, as they are meant in the literature (i.e., being asymptotically flat), violate the DEC.

Corollary IV.37
.

R
-Warp models violate the DEC.

Proof.

By the positive energy theorem
IV.20
, we know that an
R
-Warp model that satisfies the DEC (due to asymptotic flatness), is identically Minkowski. But, if one insists that it is not Minkowski, then this means that the DEC should be violated, by de Morgan’s laws of negation of conjunction.
∎

The
DEC
, in the form (
4.8
), is defined by the requirement that the measured momentum density (or energy-momentum flux) must be causal and oriented in the direction of an observer’s proper time, a principle traditionally interpreted as a prohibition against the superluminal propagation of energy (cf., e.g.,
(
Wald 1984
, Section 9.2)
,
(
Curiel 2017
, Section 2.1)
, and
(
Kontou and Sanders 2020
, Section 2.1)
).
This condition is foundational for deriving “energy estimates,” which is used to prove that a system has a well-posed IVF (or to prove the positive energy theorem; cf.
Theorem

IV.20
). However, the DEC is not strictly necessary for the IVF results. We explain this in the following Remark.

Remark IV.38
.

In an intriguing work,
Geroch 2011
specifically proposes a
democracy of causal cones
, arguing that different physical systems (modeled as symmetric hyperbolic equations) may possess their own unique causal cones that exist outside the standard light cone without invalidating the structural components of relativity. That is, the causal cones of some yet-unknown matter fields could be wider than that of the light cone. However, current experience dictates that the light cone wins the election of the democracy of causal cones mentioned above, largely because many common systems (such as electromagnetism and Dirac fields) happen to share that specific “commonality” (see
Geroch 2011
).
Nonetheless, upon this work,
Earman 2014
argues that a well-posed IVF is a more appropriate method for expressing causal limits, particularly because physical systems can maintain a maximum propagation speed equal to that of light even when they violate the DEC (see
Curiel 2017
for more details).

Error 31
.

Fuchs et al. 2024
talk about a “rule of thumb” that
“the Eulerian momentum flux and pressures should be less than the energy density to satisfy the energy conditions,” and refer to their previous work
Helmerich et al. 2024
, implying that they found this “rule of thumb” by numerical trail and error in a heuristic way (cf.
33
). However, this is a well-known fact just explained above.
A similar statement was done by
Lentz 2021a
, where the author talks about the “momentum conditions,” and compares them with the WEC. However, “momentum conditions” are nothing but the momentum constraint equation (
2.55
), and have different epistemic status than the WEC or any other energy conditions.

IV.8.4
Strong energy condition

Unlike other energy conditions, the
SEC
is routinely violated on cosmological scales by hypotheses in the standard model of cosmology such as inflation and the dark energy currently driving the Universe’s accelerated expansion.
27
27

27

The SEC is described as potentially “gerrymandered” by the relativity community because its physical significance is “obscure at best” and its primary impact is largely limited to proving singularity theorems, see
(
Curiel 2017
, Section 3.1)
.
Therefore, the violation of the SEC might not be considered as bad as the violation of other energy conditions.

In the case of
R
-Warp models,
Theorem

IV.31
offers a straightforward conclusion: if one insists that the WEC should hold, then the SEC is certainly violated for
R
-Warp models.

IV.9
Miscellaneous

In this section, we gather some Errors which we could not systematically classify above.

Error 32
.

Fell and Heisenberg 2021
apply the Helmholtz decomposition theorem to the shift vector to decompose it into a scalar and a solenoidal field on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, without specifying how regular these fields should be; indeed, the Helmholtz decomposition theorem requires the scalar field to be
C^{2}
, as they have in mind a classical (and not a distributional) formulation (cf.
IV.29
). However, later in
(
Fell and Heisenberg 2021
, Equation (10))
they introduce a scalar field that is not even
C^{1}
, as they explicitly mention this (see
(
Santiago et al. 2022
, Appendix C)
for more critiques), though they introduce a
C^{2}
function afterwards “in the interest of constructing a physically interesting” model (on
\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
). But, this is independent of physicality, it is just self-inconsistent, thereby constituting another instance of a violation of
I.1
.

Error 33
.

Many works suffer from vague and wrong presentation including
Lentz 2021a
,
Bobrick and Martire 2021
,
Garattini and Zatrimaylov 2024
(and other works by the same authors),
Helmerich et al. 2024
, and
Fuchs et al. 2024
phrase main ideas solely in a heuristic way in words without any formal description,
and make several mistakes which, at a first glance, might look like typographical ones, however, they reveal a deeper level of misunderstanding; for example,

(
i
)

taking a naive Newtonian point of view (as pointed out by
Santiago et al. 2022
), in particular regarding the definitions of total energy and momentum,

(
ii
)

the covariant
4
-velocity (unit normal vector) is taken to be past-directed without any explanation, that is indeed inconsistent in their context,

(
iii
)

throughout the work by
Fuchs et al. 2024
a blatant mistake is repeated, hinting at a basic misunderstanding, where a vector (or covector) is set to be equal to a scalar field (e.g.,
u_{i}/u^{0}=v_{s}
, in their notation, which would imply
v_{s}\equiv 0
for the Alcubierre warp drive, see
13
),

(
iv
)

the metric given by
Fuchs et al. 2024
is not spherically symmetric,

(
v
)

mixing the orthonormal and coordinate bases by
Fuchs et al. 2024
,

(
vi
)

identifying the flatness with vanishing of spatial derivatives of the metric by
Fuchs et al. 2024
.

Error 34
.

In a recent work,
White et al. 2025
concerned themselves with the “engineering-oriented design” aspects of the warp bubble that lead to a different form of warp bubble than the Alcubierre one, thereby neglecting all real pathologies of such model discussed in the present work and elsewhere, as if this was the major problem. This points again (at best) at a Newtonian understanding of the problem, hence making several mistakes and creating some misunderstandings, e.g.,
(i)
giving a wrong expression for the extrinsic curvature of the Alcubierre warp drive written below
(
White et al. 2025
, Equation (14))
(cf. (
3.47a
)),
(ii)
discussing the role of the shear tensor in a flat region as “a diagnostic of interior flatness,”
(iii)
presuming the existence or the ability of producing “exotic energy,”
(iv)
vague formulation, e.g., “the Lorentz factor between observers at rest in the ADM slicing and the physical flow of spacetime described by the shift field,” and
(v)
discussing the role of the “York time” and distinguishing it from the mean curvature (trace of the extrinsic curvature), which might be important in the so-called constant mean curvature (CMC) slicing, that is irrelevant in this context anyway.

Error 35
.

Although the work by
Clough et al. 2024
represents perhaps the most advanced numerical contribution (and the only sound numerical GR work) in the field of warp drive, it suffers from a fallacy: the “mind projection fallacy”
Jaynes 1990
; indeed, the authors claim that the gravitational waves, resulting from the “containment failure” of an Alcubierre warp drive used by a “post-warp civilization,” could be potentially detected by future detectors targeting higher frequencies. But, if a “pre-warp civilization” (i.e., us, and in particular
Clough et al. 2024
) can foresee the collapse of the Alcubierre warp drive, why a post-warp civilization, which presumably would possess a better theory of gravity, could not know this fact, and go beyond to construct it? Therefore, the idea that future gravitational-wave detectors should search for such signals, as a sign for “extraterrestrial life,” must not be taken seriously, even if one thinks that the Alcubierre warp drive would be physical despite all fundamental pathologies explained in this work and elsewhere.

IV.10
The warp bubble and its mechanism

The warp bubble and its mechanism proposed in the recent claims about physical warp drives by
Lentz 2021a
;
Bobrick and Martire 2021
;
Fell and Heisenberg 2021
and others, are at best verbally formulated, and when they try to model it mathematically, the result sometimes even fails to be a solution to the Einstein equations (see, e.g., Errors
16
and
18
).

In this section, we point out some of these misconceptions.

Remark IV.39
.

In the majority of works, the whole warp-drive mechanism is associated to the expansion tensor (or the extrinsic curvature) and its rate. However, the zero-expansion Natário warp drive could serve as a hint that it is not clear what the mechanism is supposed to be; indeed, since if the expansion could be zero, then one can ask: “what is driving the warp bubble?”

Error 36
.

Lentz 2021a
and
Fell and Heisenberg 2021
identify warp drives with “solitons.” However, it is not clear what is exactly meant by a soliton; a “classic” solitary wave, or a gravitational soliton? In either case, a warp drive
cannot
be, or at least, has
not
been shown to be associated with solitons; indeed, from a classical perspective, they lack several fundamental properties, such as the general ability
(i)
to maintain their shape if they are characterized by the expansion tensor and its rate,
(ii)
hence, to be stable,
(iii)
to have maximal set of conserved quantities, and from the gravitational point of view these solutions have not been generated by an
inverse scattering method
applied to Einstein’s equations, regardless of other (yet-to-be-shown) properties (cf., e.g.,
Belinski and Verdaguer 2001
for more details on gravitational solitons), unless they are supposed to be static or stationary vacuum solutions, i.e., black hole solutions, in which case they cease to be a warp drive (cf.
11
). This provides yet another example of violation of
I.1
.

Error 37
.

Fuchs et al. 2024
call a solution to the
3+1
-form of the geodesic equation warp drive, without exactly saying in what way it differs from the motion of a particle observed from a given reference frame, apart from a very heuristically and verbally phrased program (cf. Errors
13
and
33
).

Therefore, it is not clear what a warp bubble, hence a warp drive, is supposed to be (
III.1
provides merely a general geometric description for a warp bubble) and how it is supposed to work, quite on the contrary to the statement done by
Clough et al. 2024
in their abstract that “despite originating in science fiction, warp drives have a concrete description in general relativity.”

V
Discussion and conclusions

The goal of the present work was to demystify some ambiguities based on fundamental misconceptions, misunderstandings, and mistakes in the field of warp-drive spacetimes, in terms of Errors
1
–
37
, to show that many pseudo-problems are caused by violation of
I.1
, i.e., they have emerged from an inconsistent and vague formulation of the problem as well as improper application of GR.

These errors occurred especially in recent works where it is claimed that a physical warp drive is feasible. We summarize the most important ones in the following (one needs to add to them the errors reported by
Santiago et al. 2022
):

(
i
)

Two works by
Lentz 2021a
;
Lentz 2021b
: these works initiated the idea of possibility of constructing physical warp drives that suffer from many errors which we listed in Errors
2
,
3
,
9
,
14
,
16
,
17
,
19
,
24
,
28
,
30
,
31
,
33
,
36
.

(
ii
)

Series of works by
Bobrick and Martire 2021
;
Helmerich et al. 2024
,
Fuchs et al. 2024
: these papers encompass the widest variety of errors which can be easily refuted by applying GR adequately. We listed them in Errors
2
,
4
,
8
,
9
,
10
,
11
,
12
,
13
,
14
,
18
,
19
,
20
,
27
,
28
,
31
,
33
,
37
.

(
iii
)

Series of works by
Abellán et al. 2023a
;
Abellán et al. 2024
;
Abellán et al. 2023b
, and
Bolívar et al. 2025
: a naive evaluation of the components of the Einstein tensor is performed in these works without addressing the main pathologies of such models while making various errors (see also critiques by
Santiago et al. 2022
on the works on which this series of works is based): Errors
11
,
19
,
20
,
21
,
22
,
23
,
28
.

(
iv
)

The work by
Fell and Heisenberg 2021
: this work also contains several errors that we addressed in Errors
4
,
9
,
11
,
12
,
19
,
24
,
27
,
28
,
32
,
36
.

One has to add to this list works that appeared in the aerospace communities. We only addressed one written by members of one of these communities
in
34
.

In all these works, the given descriptions are very vague, and mostly verbally, not even formally, prescribed (cf.
33
), especially in works by
Lentz 2021a
;
Bobrick and Martire 2021
,
Fell and Heisenberg 2021
,
Helmerich et al. 2024
, and
Fuchs et al. 2024
,
Abellán et al. 2023a
;
Abellán et al. 2023b
;
Bolívar et al. 2025
, which caused an unrealistic hype in both scientific and nonscientific communities despite all Errors mentioned in the present paper and reported elsewhere, mainly by
Santiago et al. 2022
, where the authors proved, in particular,
Theorem

IV.34
showing that
R
-Warp models are fundamentally unphysical by violating the NEC. This leads us to recognize at least two issues on two different but related levels:

(
i
)

These works have been embraced by some aerospace communities, in spite of the fact that we do not even know what a warp bubble and its mechanism are supposed to be (cf.
Section

IV.10
), and the current proposals are in the most optimistic scenario consistent, but empty from any (known) physical content.

(
ii
)

As mentioned in
Section

I
, we restricted ourselves to works that have been peer-reviewed and found their way into academic journals in the field of GR.

Both these might spark potential research ideas in sociology of science, which certainly goes beyond the scope of the present work, and lies outside our expertise.

Notwithstanding, we do appreciate the curiosity to speculate and investigate warp-drive spacetimes as a possibility for FTL travels, however with the following philosophy spelled out in, e.g.,
Lobo 2017
;
Lobo and Visser 2004a
: warp-drive spacetimes are useful and interesting as Gedankenexperiments to understand and probe GR better because of their seemingly simple structure, but sometimes counterintuitive results.

Following this philosophy, to enrich such Gedankenexperiments and our understanding, in
Barzegar and Buchert 2025
we recently proposed to drop some of the restrictions listed in
Section

III.1
, most importantly
R1
, that is allowing for a tilted warp bubble which in turn allows for nonzero covariant acceleration and vorticity. Accordingly, we proposed that
R2
could be relaxed as well, allowing for general kinematics. On top of this,
R3
could also be dropped, i.e., allowing for spatial curvature in the given foliation.

In this work, we saw that another (reasonable) restriction, i.e.,
R4
, and the assumption of global hyperbolicity can be lifted:

(
i
)

Asymptotic flatness (
R4
): this puts a strong constraint on warp-drive spacetimes as shown in
Section

IV.2
(see also, e.g.,
Tipler 1976
). While this assumption is physically reasonable (see, e.g.,
(
Misner et al. 1973
, Setion 19.4)
), an immediate consequence of its lift would be that the violation of the NEC might be avoided since this restriction plays a fundamental role in
Theorem

IV.34
. An example of a reasonable scenario without
R4
would be that of a warp drive in a cosmological context, in particular, with closed spatial hypersurfaces.

(
ii
)

Global hyperbolicity: as we showed in
Theorem

IV.7
this assumption forbids construction of any
R
-Warp models. This, in a sense, supports the similar but different result by
Low 1999
. We also argued in
IV.9
that the omission of this assumption might be a necessary condition to allow for construction of a warp drive in terms of agency.

We, however, warn that any attempt to construct such Gedankenexperiments should satisfy
I.1
, and should not be taken for granted as fulfilling some physical requirements such as energy conditions, let alone being used for speculative technological applications.

Appendix A
Conformally flat metrics

Let
\bm{h}
be a
3
-dimensional Riemannian metric on a smooth Riemannian manifold
\Sigma_{t}
that foliates a Lorentzian manifold
\mathcal{M}
and assume there exists a smooth function
\psi:\Sigma_{t}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle{\bm{h}}=e^{2\psi}\bm{\gamma}\,,

(A.1)

where
\bm{\gamma}
is a flat metric.
Then,
\bm{h}
is said to be a conformal flat metric. Note that the function
\psi
is defined on
\Sigma_{t}
for each time
t
, hence a function on
\mathcal{M}
in general. We gather some useful relations in this appendix (cf., e.g.,
(
Besse 1987
, Chapter 1, J.)
,
(
Choquet-Bruhat 2008
, Appendix VI)
,
(
Baumgarte and Shapiro 2010
, Section 3.1)
,
(
Gourgoulhon 2012
, Chapter 7)
). The Levi-Civita connection coefficients associated to
\bm{h}
read

\mathchoice{\Gamma[{\bm{h}}]^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jk}}}}{\Gamma[{\bm{h}}]^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jk}}}}{\Gamma[{\bm{h}}]^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jk}}}}{\Gamma[{\bm{h}}]^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jk}}}}=\mathchoice{\Gamma[{\bm{\gamma}}]^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jk}}}}{\Gamma[{\bm{\gamma}}]^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jk}}}}{\Gamma[{\bm{\gamma}}]^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jk}}}}{\Gamma[{\bm{\gamma}}]^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jk}}}}+2\partial_{(j}\psi\,\delta^{i}_{k)}-\gamma_{jk}\gamma^{i\ell}\partial_{\ell}\psi\,,

(A.2)

where
\mathchoice{\Gamma[{\bm{\gamma}}]^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}}{\Gamma[{\bm{\gamma}}]^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}}{\Gamma[{\bm{\gamma}}]^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}}{\Gamma[{\bm{\gamma}}]^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}}
are the connection coefficients of the flat metric
\bm{\gamma}
.
The Ricci tensor associated to
\bm{h}
takes the following form

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{ij}

\displaystyle=-\left(\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\psi-\mathchoice{\Gamma[{\bm{\gamma}}]^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}}{\Gamma[{\bm{\gamma}}]^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}}{\Gamma[{\bm{\gamma}}]^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}}{\Gamma[{\bm{\gamma}}]^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}}\partial_{k}\psi-\partial_{i}\psi\partial_{j}\psi\right)

(A.3)

\displaystyle-\left(\Delta_{{\bm{\gamma}}}\psi+\left|\bm{\partial}\psi\right|^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}}\right)\gamma_{ij}\,,

where
\Delta_{\bm{\gamma}}
is the Laplacian with respect to
\bm{\gamma}
and
\left|\bm{\partial}\psi\right|^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}}=\left|\bm{\partial}\psi\right|_{\bm{\delta}}^{2}=\delta^{ij}\partial_{i}\psi\partial_{j}\psi
. Therefore, the Ricci scalar reads

\mathcal{R}=-2e^{-2\psi}\left(2\Delta_{{\bm{\gamma}}}\psi+\left|\bm{\partial}\psi\right|^{2}_{\bm{\delta}}\right)\,.

(A.4)

Moreover, the Laplacian
\Delta_{\bm{h}}
on functions
\Phi
on
\Sigma_{t}
has the following behavior

\Delta_{\bm{h}}\Phi=e^{-2\psi}\left(\Delta_{{\bm{\gamma}}}\Phi+\delta^{ij}\partial_{i}\psi\partial_{j}\Phi\right)\,.

(A.5)

Appendix B
Solving the TOV equation

In this appendix, we elaborate on
18
.

Fuchs et al. 2024
propose a process to construct a stable shell matter. We show that their process fails already in the first step as it is not a solution to the Einstein equations (the very same mistake was done by
Bobrick and Martire 2021
as pointed out correctly by
Santiago et al. 2022
).

We start with the general static, spherically symmetric metric (cf.
(
Carroll 2004
, Section 5.8)
, which is used by
Fuchs et al. 2024
, see also
33
)

\bm{g}=-e^{2a(r)}{\mathbf{d}}t^{2}+e^{2b(r)}{\mathbf{d}}r^{2}+r^{2}\bm{g}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\,,

(B.1)

where
\bm{g}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
is the round metric on the unit 2-sphere. In the first step of their process, they consider a shell with inner and outer radii
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
, respectively, with a constant density
\rho
that gives rise to the total mass
M
, i.e.,

\rho=\begin{cases}0\,;&0\leq r<R_{1}\,,\\
3M/[4\pi(R_{2}^{3}-R_{1}^{3})]\,;&R_{1}\leq r\leq R_{2}\,,\\
0\,;&r>R_{2}\,.\end{cases}

Moreover, they assume that the pressure
P
is positive and isotropic, and vanishes for
r<R_{1}
and
r=R_{2}
. With this setup they continue their speculation and try to solve this problem numerically. However, these pieces of information are enough to conclude that their process is ill-defined; indeed, since this is not a solution to the Einstein equation. The reason is that they analyze the TOV equation without respecting the Einstein equation. To show this, we start with evaluating the time-time component of the Einstein equation which results in
e^{-2b}=1-2Gm(r)/r
with
m(r)=4\pi\int_{R_{1}}^{r}\rho(s)s^{2}{\mathrm{d}}s
(cf.
(
Carroll 2004
, Equation (5.143))
). The first Darmois–Lanczos–Israel junction condition (cf., e.g.,
(
Poisson 2004
, Section 3.7)
and
Mansouri and Khorrami 1996
) implies that the induced metric on the shell surface should match that of Schwarzschild at
r=R_{2}
, hence

e^{2a(R_{2})}=1-2\frac{GM}{R_{2}}\,,

(B.2)

since
M=m(R_{2})
is the Schwarzschild mass, and it should match the Minkowski metric at
r=R_{1}
, i.e.,

a(R_{1})=0\,.

(B.3)

Now, integrating the TOV equation (
(
Carroll 2004
, Equation (5.153))
)

\frac{{\mathrm{d}}P}{{\mathrm{d}}r}=-(\rho+P)\frac{{\mathrm{d}}a}{{\mathrm{d}}r}\,,

(B.4)

results in

\ln\left[\frac{\rho}{\rho+P(r)}\right]=a(r)-a(R_{2})\,,

(B.5)

since
\rho
is constant. Evaluating the above at
r=R_{1}
we find

\frac{P(R_{1})}{\rho}=\sqrt{1-2\frac{GM}{R_{2}}}-1<0\,,

(B.6)

which not only violates the SEC (and possibly other energy conditions), but also contradicts their assumption of positive pressure in the first step (see
(
Fuchs et al. 2024
, Figure 4)
, where there is a misprint for the initial quantities and the “smoothed” ones).
